<artifact_info>
id: art__skdlFp_o3e9
type: experiment
title: Where an algorithm ban sends an AI coder
summary: |-
  Behavioural screen on Qwen/Qwen3-8B (thinking off, vLLM 0.11, T=1.0) of two predictors of where probability mass goes when a prompt bans an algorithm. The proposed predictor is substitution by association: the model's own Borda-aggregated 'standard alternative to X' ranking. The baseline is the typicality ladder: baseline frequency renormalised over the surviving modes. Two path-search tasks are used: T1 unweighted grid path (6 modes) and T2 weighted shortest path (5 modes). The artifact provides:
  (1) A reusable task suite: specs, 48 + 45 seeded hidden tests with per-case time limits, 22 reference implementations, and a sandbox harness.
  (2) A validated labeller: runtime tracer + AST rules + a strategy-blind gpt-4.1-mini judge on disagreements, with a gold set adjudicated by gpt-4.1-mini, gemini-2.5-flash and deepseek tie-break. Both labellers get 22/22 references right; on evaluation gold, kappa runtime-vs-AST is 0.753, runtime vs adjudicated 0.843, combined label 0.966. gate_passed=false is flagged.
  (3) data/samples.jsonl: 3,260 labelled programs in the shared screen schema (task, condition, paraphrase, model, decoding, label, passed, code, ...), poolable with other experiments.
  (4) Frozen, sha256 hash-chained predictions (commitments.log); every event was committed before its generation.
  (5) method_out.json (exp_gen_sol_out) with one example per event plus all analyses in metadata.
  Key results, descriptive only (2 tasks, 2 informative events):
  - Baselines are fully collapsed (T1 100% BFS, T2 100% Dijkstra, also at T=1.3), so the frequency predictor is uniform and its nomination is always tied.
  - Modal bans were obeyed (compliance 0.90 / 0.91) and landed entirely on one substitute: DFS for T1 (38/38 compliant correct samples), Bellman-Ford/SPFA for T2 (86/86).
  - Association hit T2 but missed T1, where the Borda map nominated Dijkstra with clarity 0.20 and ranked DFS last. Log scores were T1 -2.82 vs -1.63 and T2 -0.75 vs -1.41; mean paired difference -0.27 nats/sample; association wins 1 of 2.
  - The model's own free-text 'standard alternative' answers and the listall typicality cue nominated the realised substitute in both events (2/2). A post-hoc gpt-4.1-mini map shows the same ranking-vs-free-text split.
  - Cumulative double bans failed (compliance 0.06 / 0.00): the model re-implemented the banned mechanism under other names ('backtracking' DFS, 'BFS with pruning' = SPFA). 41/94 and 58/100 recurrent programs never name the banned algorithm, so the succession stopped at step 2.
  - Mention twins act as strong recommendations (+56 to +100 pp for 5 modes; 0 for 3), not placebos.
  All 36 headline numbers were independently re-derived from raw files (audit/rederive.py), and placebo runs of the contest and T9 comparisons fail as expected. reproducibility.md gives an exact CPU-only Path A (byte-identical outputs verified, no API or GPU calls) and a full-regeneration Path B. OpenRouter spend was USD 0.27. Next-round implications: the ranking instrument diverges from free-text association; frequency-based typicality is uninformative under full collapse, while listall typicality is informative; multi-name bans need mechanism-level compliance checks.
workspace_path: >-

<data_loading_pattern>
`mini_demo_data.json` = curated subset for the demo.
Use this pattern for Colab compatibility (GitHub URL with local fallback):
```python
GITHUB_DATA_URL = "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ai-inventor-papers/ai-invention-295f8e-prompt-bans-substitute-not-just-subtract/fork/run_jSassd-YWLVC/round-1/experiment-1/demo/mini_demo_data.json"
import http.client
import json
import urllib.request
from pathlib import Path

def load_data():
    try:
        with urllib.request.urlopen(GITHUB_DATA_URL, timeout=15) as response:
            return json.loads(response.read().decode())
    # Network errors, timeouts, a placeholder URL, bad JSON, a cut-off response.
    except (OSError, ValueError, http.client.HTTPException) as e:
        print("GitHub data unavailable, using local mini_demo_data.json:", e)
    local = Path("mini_demo_data.json")
    if local.exists(): return json.loads(local.read_text())
    raise FileNotFoundError("Could not load mini_demo_data.json")
```
</data_loading_pattern>

take this repo as inspiration: https://github.com/DrejcPesjak/llm-creativity
use one or more open-weight models (minimum 1B params), probably instruction tuned it
and first run behavioral analysis (similar to repo mentioned above), then move to interpretability (can be manifolds, or also other techniques like sae, circuits, nla, j-lens, logit lens...)
the question is: how does "creativity" manifest inside the model, and can we influence it?
(in layman terms, can we make llms give truly creative and novel solutions?)

(you do not need to implement everything bellow, see whats reasonable, interesting and might lead to new insights)
is "temp" really the "llm creativity knob"?  (Astra found "coincidentally" similar direction for navier-stokes solution, as Buckmaster & Levent)
(always same seed)
1 a) does a tight temp , with topk=10 produce 10 reasoning trajectories of 1 idea/solution but same 10 phrasings
b) or does tight temp, with topk=10 produce 10 reasoning trajectories of 10 ideas/solutions with 1 phrasing each
c) and does loosing up temp, make a) more to 2/3/4 ideas each with 10 phrasings ; or make b) keep original 10 ideas, now each with 10 phrasings
(temp is rather a minor part in this idea, put more focus on the following things)
test: "implement path saerch algorithm" -> does llm always design BFS?
2 can context seeding help? prepand "seed=d32qxk4nt43" or "seed=(horse chair roof love)"
hyp: is llm a probablistic model, and thus gives you the average/mean answer? and only context pushes you into a different subspace
 - ie of possible range of answer [0,100] , on direct question llm gives you 50. then with context you move to [80,100] subspace and llm gives you 90
3 so how much does prompt instructions matter? ("Make a new algorithm", "Make a fully novel algorithm never seen before ... 2000 words ..."
-> analyze the internals , all the runs that produced BFS , vs all runs that produced DFS , vs dijkstra ...
  -> analyze with thought anchors, NLA, SAE ...
  -> what is the average answer? plot a "path search algorithm" manifold, color each algorithm centroid by implemented-count (lets say we have 10 algorithms in total of 100 gens)
     -> then prohibit the top 5 in prompt, and see if the implemented count moves to the edge of those 5, or to the opposite side (if its a circle manifold)
  - (manifold lit: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.05115 , https://www.goodfire.com/research/manifold-steering ) - geodesic and mds
    - (the reports need to contain actual geometry plots, actual activation space, not some summary bar charts)

{"stdout": "# What does a ban switch the model to?\n\nA behavioural screen on **Qwen/Qwen3-8B** (thinking off, vLLM 0.11.0, T = 1.0) of two rival predictors of where\nprobability mass goes when a prompt bans an algorithm:\n\n- **Substitution by association** (the proposed method): the model's own ranked \"standard alternative to X\" map,\n  elicited with 20 permuted-order complete rankings per catalogue mode and Borda-aggregated.\n- **Typicality ladder** (the baseline/rival): the latent baseline frequency of each mode, renormalised over the\n  modes that survive the ban.\n\nTwo path-search tasks are used: T1, an unweighted grid path with 6 catalogue modes, and T2, a weighted shortest path\nwith 5 modes. Every prediction is frozen and sha256 hash-chained (`commitments.log`) **before** the samples it\npredicts\nare generated. All results are descriptive: two tasks and two informative events cannot support an inferential claim.\n\n## Headline results (all numbers in `method_out.json` / `results/`)\n\n| | T1 grid path | T2 weighted shortest path |\n|---|---|---|\n| Baseline (N=300, also T=1.3 and no neutral segment) | 100% BFS | 100% Dijkstra |\n| Single ban of the modal mode | compliance 0.90 → all 38 compliant correct samples are **DFS** | compliance 0.91 → all 86 compliant correct samples are **Bellman-Ford/SPFA** |\n| Association (Borda) nomination | Dijkstra (clarity 0.20; DFS ranked last) → **miss** | Bellman-Ford/SPFA → **hit** |\n| Frequency (typicality) nomination | tied over all survivors (baseline fully collapsed) | tied |\n| Free-text \"standard alternative\" / listall cue | DFS / DFS → hit / hit | Bellman-Ford/SPFA / Bellman-Ford/SPFA → hit / hit |\n| Cumulative double ban (succession step 2) | compliance **0.06**; 41/94 recurrent programs never name the banned algorithm (\"backtracking\") | compliance **0.00**; 58/100 recurrent programs never name it (\"BFS with pruning\" = SPFA) |\n\n- **Contest result.** Association beat frequency on 1 of 2 informative events. The mean paired log-score difference\n  was −0.27 nats/sample. Within-event CIs are degenerate because every compliant sample in each event has the same\n  label. The model's own free-text answer and the \"list all approaches\" typicality cue nominated the realised\n  substitute in both events.\n- **Post-hoc textbook map.** gpt-4.1-mini (`elicitation/ref_assoc_objects.json`), collected after the freeze and\n  so not a contest predictor, shows the same split for X = BFS: its Borda winner is bidirectional BFS, while its\n  free-text answer is DFS. The ranking question appears to elicit the closest shortest-path variant rather than\n  the canonical \"standard alternative\".\n- **Succession.** Both successions stopped at step 2 under the pre-registered fewer-than-20-compliant rule: with two\n  banned names the model re-implements the banned mechanism under another description.\n- **Mention twins are not placebos.** Mentioning an absent mode moves +56 to +100 pp of mass to it (DFS, IDDFS,\n  A*, Bellman-Ford/SPFA, Floyd-Warshall), but about 0 pp for Dijkstra on grids, Dial and bidirectional Dijkstra.\n- **Sham bans.** Banning an absent mode leaves the modal mode at 77–100%. It shifts up to 23% of mass toward a\n  related mode: a Floyd-Warshall ban gives 23% Bellman-Ford/SPFA.\n- **Labeller.** Both labellers identify all 22 reference variants. The gate's runtime-accuracy targets were **not**\n  met on the evaluation gold: kappa runtime-vs-AST 0.75, runtime vs adjudicated 0.84, AST vs adjudicated 0.89,\n  combined label vs adjudicated 0.97. Per protocol, every runtime/AST disagreement is labelled by the blind judge,\n  and `gate_passed=false` is recorded.\n- **Cost.** OpenRouter USD 0.27 in total (judge, gold adjudication, Llama + gpt-4.1-mini elicitation); about 6 GPU-minutes of generation on one RTX 5090.\n\n## Layout\n\n| Path | What it is |\n|---|---|\n| `method.py` | Orchestrator; one sub-command per stage (see \"How to run\"). |\n| `pipeline.py` | Stage code for the task suite, labelling (extract → tests + probes → AST/runtime → judge → combine) and the gate dispatcher. |\n| `common.py` | Frozen protocol constants: task texts, catalogue and display names, thresholds, the hash-chain commit helper. |\n| `gen/prompts.py` | Builds every paraphrase of every condition and the elicitation prompts. |\n| `gen/run_vllm.py` | Async batch runner against one `vllm serve` process: chat template with `enable_thinking=False`, prompts sent as token ids, per-request seeds. |\n| `prompts/conditions.json` | All frozen prompts (sha256-committed before any generation). |\n| `tasks/{t1_grid,t2_wsp}/spec.txt` | Task specification (DESCRIPTION + FORMAT). |\n| `tasks/{t1_grid,t2_wsp}/references/*.py` | 12 + 10 reference implementations, at least 2 variants per catalogue mode. |\n| `tasks/{t1_grid,t2_wsp}/tests.json`, `limits.json` | Seeded hidden tests (48 / 45 cases) and per-case time limits (max(1 s, 10 × the slowest reference)). |\n| `tasks/tests_gen.py` | Hidden-test generators and checkers. |\n| `tasks/sandbox_tests.py` | Fresh-process test runner: stdlib-only CPython `-I -S`, 2 GB RLIMIT_AS, 256 MB thread stack, forbidden-import screen. |\n| `tasks/harness.py` | Parent-side subprocess harness. |\n| `labeller/extract.py` | Code-block extraction. |\n| `labeller/ast_rules.py` | Static labeller. |\n| `labeller/tracer.py` | Runtime tracer subprocess (heapq / deque / queue proxies, list-pop direction, grid-access order, line scaling). |\n| `labeller/runtime_rules.py` | Runtime decision rules. |\n| `labeller/judge.py` | Strategy-blind OpenRouter judge with cost ledger and budget stop. |\n| `labeller/combine.py` | Combines runtime, AST and judge labels. |\n| `labeller/gate.py` | Labeller gate. |\n| `labeller/gold_set.jsonl`, `labeller/gate_report.json` (+ `_round0`) | Adjudicated gold set and gate metrics. |\n| `labeller/judge_cache.jsonl` | Every judge response, keyed by model and code sha256. |\n| `elicitation/*.jsonl`, `elicitation/assoc_objects.json`, `elicitation/baseline_objects.json` | Qwen and Llama-3.1-8B rankings and free-text answers; Borda / first-rank / clarity / Kendall's W maps; baseline frequencies and strata. |\n| `predictions/*.json`, `commitments.log` | Frozen composed predictions and their sha256 hash chain. |\n| `data/raw_*.jsonl` | Raw generations (pre-ban, ban arms, succession, ranking retries, smoke, reproducibility check). |\n| `data/labelled.jsonl` | Every program row with code, tests, runtime/AST/judge labels and raw text. |\n| `data/samples.jsonl` | One row per generated program in the shared screen schema (3,260 rows). |\n| `data/program_eval_cache.jsonl` | Per-unique-program test results and raw tracer probes. |\n| `data/condition_summaries.json` | Per-condition label counts, pass rates (Wilson), Hill_cc, other / no-code / truncation rates. |\n| `analysis/assoc.py`, `analysis/stages.py`, `analysis/succession.py` | Association objects, prediction composition, freeze and ban/succession stages. |\n| `analysis/metrics.py`, `analysis/score.py`, `analysis/outputs.py` | Statistics and scoring, `method_out.json` and figures. |\n| `results/*.json` | Event table, contest, T9 family test, S1 sham control (+ sham-vs-baseline), mention effects, C4, landing table, commitment audit, disguised recurrence, gate, task-suite acceptance, OpenRouter cost ledger, seed reproducibility check. |\n| `figures/*.png` | Predicted-vs-realised bars, paired log scores, landing chart. |\n| `method_out.json` (+ `full_` / `mini_` / `preview_`) | Final output in the `exp_gen_sol_out` schema: one example per event plus the reference conditions and mention twins. |\n| `logs/` | Stage logs and the vLLM server log, which shows the effective SamplingParams of every request. |\n| `audit/rederive.py`, `results/independent_rederivation.json` | Independent re-derivation of the 36 headline numbers from raw files, through separate code, plus placebo runs of the contest and T9 comparisons (36/36 match; placebos fail as they should). |\n| `reproducibility.md`, `restore.sh` | Step-by-step reproduction: Path A (exact, CPU only, from the published raw generations) and Path B (full regeneration); venv restore. |\n| `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock` | All 167 installed packages pinned with `==` (from `uv pip freeze`), with the matching linux x86_64 lock. |\n\nEverything stays on the run's volume; nothing here is 100 MB or larger, so everything is published except\n`.venv/`.\n\n## How to run\n\nSee `reproducibility.md` for the complete, ordered procedure. It covers exact CPU-only re-analysis from the\npublished raw generations, and full regeneration.\n\nFull regeneration requires a GPU with at least 20 GB (32 GB used here), `uv`, and the OpenRouter env vars\n(`OPENROUTER_BASE_URL`, `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`). Candidate programs run in a stdlib-only CPython 3.12: set\n`SANDBOX_PYTHON` if `python3.12` is not at `/usr/local/bin` or `/usr/bin`.\n\n```bash\nuv venv .venv --python=3.12 && uv pip install --python .venv/bin/python -r pyproject.toml   # or: ./restore.sh\n# one long-lived server for every GPU stage (no C compiler in this container -> --enforce-eager)\n.venv/bin/vllm serve Qwen/Qwen3-8B --dtype bfloat16 --gpu-memory-utilization 0.90 --max-model-len 2048 \\\n  --max-num-seqs 128 --seed 1234 --generation-config vllm --enable-prefix-caching --enforce-eager \\\n  --port 8011 --host 127.0.0.1 --served-model-name qwen3-screen --enable-log-requests --max-log-len 120 &\nuv run method.py prompts                    # freeze + hash prompts\nuv run method.py smoke                      # template / extraction / ranking-parse smoke test\nuv run method.py preban --limit 10; uv run method.py preban --limit 100; uv run method.py preban\nuv run method.py tasks                      # hidden tests, time limits, reference + mutation acceptance\nuv run method.py label --src data/raw_preban.jsonl --no-judge   # mechanical labels only (rule development)\nuv run method.py gate                       # labeller gate (gold set adjudication)\nuv run method.py label --src data/raw_preban.jsonl              # full labelling incl. judge\nuv run method.py elicit; uv run method.py external; uv run method.py assoc\n# (optional, post hoc, descriptive) textbook-consensus map with gpt-4.1-mini, run after the freeze here:\nuv run method.py reference_map\nuv run method.py freeze                     # predictions hash-committed BEFORE any ban prompt\nuv run method.py bans                       # ban arms N=100 + mention twins N=50, then labelling\nuv run method.py succession                 # greedy cumulative bans (each step frozen before generation)\nuv run method.py score; uv run method.py outputs\n```\n\nEvery stage resumes from what is already on disk: generation skips request ids that are present, and evaluation\nand judge calls are cached by code sha256.\n\n**Reproducibility.** Per-request seeds are `int(sha256('task|condition|paraphrase|stage')[:8], 16)` and are stored in\nevery row. Re-sending 6 requests (64 samples) to the same server reproduced 39/64 texts exactly\n(`results/seed_reproducibility_check.json`). bf16 numerics depend on batch composition, so reproducibility is at the\ndistribution level, not the token level.\n\n## Deviations from the plan (also in `method_out.json` → `metadata.deviations`)\n\n- The smoke test omitted the ban prompt, so that no ban output existed before the freeze.\n- vLLM ran with `--enforce-eager` because the container has no C compiler for Triton/Inductor. Only speed is\n  affected.\n- `max_num_seqs=128` and `gpu_memory_utilization=0.90`, because the pod had a 32 GB card.\n- The first character of every condition segment is upper-cased.\n- Gate revision round 1 made the tracer pre-inject common stdlib names. It changed no gold label, and the gate\n  targets were still not met.\n- 60/440 free-text answers were truncated at 32 tokens.\n- Both baselines are fully collapsed (degenerate-baseline fallback): the frequency predictor is uniform for every\n  event, its nomination is tied/unstable, the STABLE-D-AF stratum is empty, and the subtraction family in the T9\n  test is uniform.\n- Succession stopped at step 2 for both tasks (ban failure), so there are only 2 informative contest events.\n- Gold adjudication used gpt-4.1-mini and gemini-2.5-flash, with deepseek-chat-v3.1 as tie-breaker. The production\n  judge is gpt-4.1-mini, so the combined-label gold accuracy is optimistic.\n\n## Restoring removed files\n\nOnly regenerable bulk is marked for deletion in `.aii/manifest.yaml`:\n\n- `.venv/` (about 14 GB): run `./restore.sh`, or\n  `uv venv .venv --python=3.12 && uv pip install --python .venv/bin/python -r pyproject.toml`. All 167 packages\n  are pinned exactly, e.g. `vllm==0.11.0`, `torch==2.8.0`, `transformers==4.57.6`.\n- `__pycache__/`, `analysis/__pycache__/`, `gen/__pycache__/`, `labeller/__pycache__/` and `tasks/__pycache__/`\n  (bytecode caches, about 50 KB to 1 MB each) are recreated automatically on import by any stage. To rebuild them\n  all at once: `uv run python -m compileall -q -l method.py pipeline.py common.py analysis gen labeller tasks`.\n- The model weights are not stored in this workspace (they live in the run's shared HF cache). )\n\n\ndef load_cost() -> dict[str, Any]:\n    if COST_FILE.exists():\n        return json.loads(COST_FILE.read_text())\n    return {\"total_usd\": 0.0, \"by_purpose\": {}, \"n_calls\": 0, 6)\n        c[\"by_purpose\"][purpose] = round(c[\"by_purpose\"].get(purpose, 0.0) + usd, 6)\n        c[\"n_calls\"] += 1\n        c[\"budget_refusals\"] += int(refused)\n        c[\"updated_utc\"] = CACHE.read_text().splitlines():\n            if line.strip():\n                try:\n                    r = json.loads(line)\n                    out[r[\"key\"]] = r\n                except json.JSONDecodeError:\n                    continue\n    return out\n\n\ndef _append_cache(rec: dict[str, Any]) -> None:\n    with _LOCK:\n        with CACHE.open(\"a\") as fh:\n            fh.write(json.dumps(rec) + \"\\n\")\n\n\ndef parse_judge(text: str, task: str) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    m = re.search(r\"\\{.*\\}\", text, re.DOTALL)\n    lbl, conf, rat = None, None, \"\"\n    if m:\n str(js.get(\"rationale\", \"\"))[:300]\n        except json.JSONDecodeError:\n            pass\n    if lbl is None:\n        mm = re.search(r'\"label\"\\s*:\\s*\"([a-z_]+)\"', text)\n        lbl = mm.group(1) if mm else None\n    valid = set(MODES[task]) | {OTHER}\n    if lbl not in valid:\n        lbl = OTHER if lbl else None\n    try:\n        conf = float(conf) if conf is not None else model.startswith(\"google/\"):\n            extra[\"reasoning\"] = {\"max_tokens\": 0}\n        for attempt in range(4):\n            if stop.get(\"stop\"):\n                return None\n            try:\n                resp = await client.chat.completions.create(\n                    model=model, messages=[{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": judge_prompt(task, code)}], temperature=0.0,\n from openai import AsyncOpenAI\n\n    client = AsyncOpenAI(base_url=os.environ[\"OPENROUTER_BASE_URL\"], api_key=os.environ[\"OPENROUTER_API_KEY\"],\n                         timeout=120.0, list[dict[str, Any]], model: str, purpose: str, cap_usd: float,\n               concurrency: int = 16) -> tuple[list[dict[str, Any] | None], dict[str, Any]]:\n    \"\"\"items: [{'task', 'code'}]; returns per-item judge records (None if not judged) and the stop state.\"\"\"\n    out, stop = asyncio.run(judge_many_async(items, model, purpose, cap_usd, concurrency))\n    logger.info(f\"judge {model} [{purpose}]: {sum(r is not None for r in out)}/{len(out)} judged; \"\n                f\"spent {purpose_spent(purpose):.4f} USD on {purpose}; stop={stop}\")\n    return out, stop\n\n\nif print('file    sha', hashlib.sha256(open('$SRC/prompts/conditions.json','rb').read()).hexdigest())
print(len(txt))
"

### [61] TOOL CALL — Write · 2026-10-26 08:48:22 UTC

```
File: /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_1/art__skdlFp_o3e9/tools/build_mini_demo_data.py

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Build mini_demo_data.json for code_demo.ipynb from the experiment folder (art__skdlFp_o3e9).

Usage:
    python3 tools/build_mini_demo_data.py --src <path to the experiment folder> [--out mini_demo_data.json]

The experiment folder is the one that holds method.py, data/raw_*.jsonl, data/labelled.jsonl,
data/program_eval_cache.jsonl, labeller/judge_cache.jsonl, predictions/, results/ and commitments.log.

Selection (systematic, no cherry-picking; rows are recorded Qwen3-8B generations):
  * baseline (t1, t2)                      sample_index 0 of each of the 10 paraphrases   -> 10 + 10
  * modal single ban (t1 ban:bfs, t2 ban:dijkstra)
                                           sample_index 0-1 of every paraphrase and
                                           sample_index 2 of paraphrases 0-4              -> 25 + 25
  * mention twin of the realised substitute (t1 mention:dfs, t2 mention:bf_spfa)
                                           sample_index 0 of paraphrases 0-4              ->  5 +  5
  * succession step 2 = cumulative double ban (t1 succession:2, t2 succession:2)
                                           sample_index 0 of each of the 10 paraphrases   -> 10 + 10
                                                                                    total    100 programs
Auxiliary records (not programs) needed to rebuild the predictors and to verify the freeze:
  * Qwen3-8B ranking + free-text elicitation rows for the two modal bans (X=BFS on t1, X=Dijkstra on t2)
  * cached program evaluations (hidden tests + raw tracer probes) and cached blind-judge labels for the 100 programs
  * the hash-chained commitment log and the raw text of the four frozen prediction files
  * full-run reference numbers (event table, contest, mention effects, disguised recurrence, label counts)
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
from collections import Counter, defaultdict
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Iterator

GROUPS = [  # (task, condition, selector(paraphrase_id, sample_index) -> bool)
    ("t1", "baseline", lambda p, s: s == 0),
    ("t2", "baseline", lambda p, s: s == 0),
    ("t1", "ban:bfs", lambda p, s: s in (0, 1) or (s == 2 and p < 5)),
    ("t2", "ban:dijkstra", lambda p, s: s in (0, 1) or (s == 2 and p < 5)),
    ("t1", "mention:dfs", lambda p, s: s == 0 and p < 5),
    ("t2", "mention:bf_spfa", lambda p, s: s == 0 and p < 5),
    ("t1", "succession:2", lambda p, s: s == 0),
    ("t2", "succession:2", lambda p, s: s == 0),
]
ELICIT = {("t1", "bfs"), ("t2", "dijkstra")}
EVENTS = ["t1|ban:bfs", "t2|ban:dijkstra", "t1|succession:2", "t2|succession:2"]
PRED_FILES = ["predictions/t1_single_bans.json", "predictions/t2_single_bans.json",
              "predictions/t1_succ_step2.json", "predictions/t2_succ_step2.json"]
JUDGE_MODEL = "openai/gpt-4.1-mini"


def jsonl(p: Path) -> Iterator[dict[str, Any]]:
    with p.open() as fh:
        for line in fh:
            line = line.strip()
            if line:
                yield json.loads(line)


def main() -> None:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--src", required=True, type=Path)
    ap.add_argument("--out", default=Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / "mini_demo_data.json", type=Path)
    a = ap.parse_args()
    src: Path = a.src

    # ---- programs -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    wanted = {(t, c): sel for t, c, sel in GROUPS}
    picked: dict[tuple[str, str], list[dict[str, Any]]] = defaultdict(list)
    rank_rows, free_rows = [], []
    for name in ("raw_preban.jsonl", "raw_bans.jsonl", "raw_succession.jsonl"):
        for r in jsonl(src / "data" / name):
            key = (r["task"], r["condition"])
            if r["kind"] == "program" and key in wanted and wanted[key](int(r["paraphrase_id"]), int(r["sample_index"])):
                picked[key].append(r)
            elif r["kind"] == "rank" and (r["task"], r["mode_X"]) in ELICIT:
                rank_rows.append(r)
            elif r["kind"] == "free" and (r["task"], r["mode_X"]) in ELICIT:
                free_rows.append(r)
    labelled = {f"{r['req_id']}|{r['sample_index']}": r for r in jsonl(src / "data" / "labelled.jsonl")}
    examples = []
    for t, c, _sel in GROUPS:
        rows = sorted(picked[(t, c)], key=lambda r: (int(r["sample_index"]), int(r["paraphrase_id"])))
        for r in rows:
            lab = labelled[f"{r['req_id']}|{r['sample_index']}"]
            ex = dict(r)
            ex["full_run"] = {k: lab.get(k) for k in ("label", "passed", "labeller_source", "runtime_label",
                                                      "ast_label", "judge_label", "eval_key", "code_sha256")}
            examples.append(ex)
    rank_rows.sort(key=lambda r: (r["task"], int(r["paraphrase_id"])))
    free_rows.sort(key=lambda r: (r["task"], int(r["sample_index"])))

    # ---- caches for exactly these programs -------------------------------------------------------------------
    keys = {ex["full_run"]["eval_key"] for ex in examples if ex["full_run"]["eval_key"]}
    eval_cache = {r["key"]: r for r in jsonl(src / "data" / "program_eval_cache.jsonl") if r["key"] in keys}
    jkeys = {f"{JUDGE_MODEL}|{ex['task']}|{ex['full_run']['code_sha256']}" for ex in examples
             if ex["full_run"]["code_sha256"]}
    judge_cache = {r["key"]: r for r in jsonl(src / "labeller" / "judge_cache.jsonl") if r["key"] in jkeys}

    # ---- freeze material and full-run reference numbers ------------------------------------------------------
    commitments = list(jsonl(src / "commitments.log"))
    frozen = {p: (src / p).read_text() for p in PRED_FILES}
    res = src / "results"
    event_table = [e for e in json.loads((res / "event_table.json").read_text()) if e["event_id"] in EVENTS]
    contest = json.loads((res / "contest.json").read_text())
    mention = json.loads((res / "mention_effects.json").read_text())
    disguised = json.loads((res / "disguised_recurrence.json").read_text())
    assoc = json.loads((src / "elicitation" / "assoc_objects.json").read_text())
    counts: dict[str, Any] = {}
    for r in labelled.values():
        k = f"{r['task']}|{r['condition']}"
        if (r["task"], r["condition"]) in wanted:
            d = counts.setdefault(k, {"n": 0, "labels": Counter(), "labels_correct": Counter()})
            d["n"] += 1
            d["labels"][r["label"]] += 1
            if r["passed"]:
                d["labels_correct"][r["label"]] += 1
    full_run = {
        "event_table": event_table,
        "contest_events": contest["events"],
        "contest_all_informative": contest["strata"]["all_informative"],
        "mention_effects": {k: mention[k] for k in ("t1|dfs", "t2|bf_spfa")},
        "disguised_recurrence": disguised,
        "condition_label_counts": {k: {"n": v["n"], "labels": dict(v["labels"]),
                                       "labels_correct": dict(v["labels_correct"])} for k, v in counts.items()},
        "qwen_assoc": {f"{t}|{x}": {kk: assoc["qwen"][t][x][kk] for kk in
                                    ("borda_points", "first_counts", "borda_winner", "first_winner", "clarity",
                                     "kendall_w", "n_rankings")} for t, x in sorted(ELICIT)},
    }
    out = {
        "metadata": {
            "title": "Where an algorithm ban sends an AI coder -- demo subset",
            "source_artifact": "art__skdlFp_o3e9 (experiment): behavioural screen on Qwen/Qwen3-8B, thinking off, "
                               "vLLM 0.11, T=1.0",
            "selection": __doc__.split("Selection")[1].split("Auxiliary")[0].strip(),
            "n_examples": len(examples),
            "examples_per_condition": {f"{t}|{c}": len(picked[(t, c)]) for t, c, _ in GROUPS},
            "elicitation": {"rank_rows": rank_rows, "free_rows": free_rows},
            "eval_cache": eval_cache,
            "judge_cache": judge_cache,
            "commitments": commitments,
            "frozen_predictions": frozen,
            "full_run": full_run,
        },
        "examples": examples,
    }
    a.out.write_text(json.dumps(out, ensure_ascii=False))
    print(f"wrote {a.out} ({a.out.stat().st_size / 1e6:.2f} MB): {len(examples)} programs, "
          f"{len(rank_rows)} ranking rows, {len(free_rows)} free-text rows, {len(eval_cache)} cached evaluations, "
          f"{len(judge_cache)} cached judge labels")
    print("per condition:", out["metadata"]["examples_per_condition"])
    sub = Counter((ex["task"], ex["condition"], ex["full_run"]["label"], ex["full_run"]["passed"]) for ex in examples)
    for k in sorted(sub):
        print("  ", k, sub[k])


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

File: /tmp/claude-10001/-ai-inventor-aii-data-runs-run-jSassd-YWLVC-4-gen-paper-repo--4-gen-demo-art-notebook-workspaces-iter-1-art--skdlFp-o3e9/9a8c7573-84b8-46aa-b67e-43ff456eeaca/scratchpad/nb/to_ipynb.py

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Convert a percent-delimited source ('# %% [markdown]' / '# %%' lines start cells) into a .ipynb file."""

import json
import sys
from pathlib import Path

src, dst = Path(sys.argv[1]), Path(sys.argv[2])
cells, cur, kind = [], [], None


def flush():
    if kind is None:
        return
    while cur and not cur[0].strip():
        cur.pop(0)
    while cur and not cur[-1].strip():
        cur.pop()
    lines = [l + "\n" for l in cur]
    if lines:
        lines[-1] = lines[-1].rstrip("\n")
    cell = {"cell_type": kind, "id": f"cell-{len(cells) + 1:02d}", "metadata": {}, "source": lines}
    if kind == "code":
        cell.update({"execution_count": None, "outputs": []})
    cells.append(cell)


for line in src.read_text().splitlines():
    if line.startswith("# %% [markdown]"):
        flush(); cur, kind = [], "markdown"
    elif line.startswith("# %%"):
        flush(); cur, kind = [], "code"
    else:
        cur.append(line)
flush()
nb = {"cells": cells, "metadata": {"kernelspec": {"display_name": "Python 3", "language": "python", "name": "python3"},
                                   "language_info": {"name": "python", "version": "3.12"}},
      "nbformat": 4, "nbformat_minor": 5}
dst.write_text(json.dumps(nb, indent=1, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
print(f"{dst}: {len(cells)} cells ({sum(c['cell_type'] == 'code' for c in cells)} code)")
```

### [89] TOOL CALL — Write · 2026-10-26 09:01:25 UTC

````
File: /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_jSassd-YWLVC/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_1/art__skdlFp_o3e9/README.md

# Where an algorithm ban sends an AI coder: demo notebook

A runnable, CPU-only walk-through of `method.py` from the experiment **"What does a ban switch the model to?"**.
The experiment is a behavioural screen on Qwen/Qwen3-8B (thinking off, vLLM 0.11, T = 1.0) of two predictors of
where probability mass goes when a prompt bans an algorithm:

- **substitution by association**: the model's own Borda-aggregated "standard alternative to X" ranking;
- **the typicality ladder**: the baseline frequency, renormalised over the surviving modes.

The screen uses two path-search tasks: T1, a grid path with 6 modes, and T2, a weighted shortest path with 5 modes.

`code_demo.ipynb` keeps the original code and splits it into cells, with explanations between them. The code comes
from `method.py` and the stage code it dispatches to: `common.py`, `gen/prompts.py`, `pipeline.py`,
`labeller/{extract,ast_rules,runtime_rules,combine,judge}.py` and `analysis/{assoc,metrics,stages,score}.py`.

The generation stages need a vLLM server with Qwen3-8B on a GPU, and the judge needs an OpenRouter key. So the
notebook follows the experiment's own *Path A* (exact re-analysis from the recorded generations): it **replays
recorded Qwen3-8B generations** and re-runs every CPU stage on them.

| Stage | In the notebook |
|---|---|
| `prompts` | rebuilds `prompts/conditions.json` and verifies its sha256 against the hash-chained commitment log (byte-identical) |
| `preban` / `bans` / `succession` | builds the original request plans and per-request seeds; replays the recorded rows, checking each row's seed |
| `label` | code extraction, then the runtime rules on cached tracer probes and the AST rules on the code, the cached blind-judge labels, and `combine` |
| `elicit` / `assoc` | ranking parsing, Borda association map, free-text mapping, baseline frequencies |
| `freeze` | sha256 check of the 4 frozen prediction files; recomputes the association / frequency / free-text predictions and compares them with the frozen ones |
| `score` | event outcomes, commitment audit, log-score contest, disguised recurrence, mention effects |

## Layout

| Path | What it is |
|---|---|
| `code_demo.ipynb` | The demo notebook, saved with the outputs of its last test run. |
| `mini_demo_data.json` | Curated demo data, about 0.6 MB (see below). The notebook loads it from GitHub and falls back to this local copy. |
| `tools/build_mini_demo_data.py` | Rebuilds `mini_demo_data.json` from the experiment folder (`--src <folder>`). |
| `README.md` | This file. |
| `.aii/manifest.yaml` | Storage decisions. Nothing in this folder is heavy, so it has no entries. |

## The demo data (`mini_demo_data.json`)

`examples` holds **100 recorded program generations**, in the raw generation schema plus each row's full-run label.
They were picked systematically, by sample index, with no selection on outcome:

| Condition | T1 | T2 |
|---|---|---|
| baseline | 10 | 10 |
| modal single ban (`ban:bfs` / `ban:dijkstra`) | 25 | 25 |
| mention twin of the realised substitute (`mention:dfs` / `mention:bf_spfa`) | 5 | 5 |
| succession step 2, a double ban (`succession:2`) | 10 | 10 |

`metadata` holds the auxiliary records the stages need:

- the 40 ranking and 40 free-text elicitation rows for X = BFS (T1) and X = Dijkstra (T2);
- the cached program evaluations (hidden tests + raw tracer probes) and the cached judge labels for these 100
  programs;
- the 5-entry commitment log;
- the raw text of the 4 frozen prediction files;
- full-run reference numbers for side-by-side comparison.

## How to run

**Colab:** open `code_demo.ipynb` and run all cells. The install cell installs only `loguru`; Colab's own numpy,
scipy, pandas and matplotlib are used.

**Locally** (Python 3.12):

```bash
uv venv .venv --seed --python 3.12
uv pip install --python .venv/bin/python jupyter ipykernel
.venv/bin/jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute code_demo.ipynb --output code_demo.ipynb
```

Off Colab, the install cell pins Colab's versions: numpy 2.0.2, scipy 1.16.3, pandas 2.2.2, matplotlib 3.10.0 and
loguru 0.7.3.

**Configuration** (the notebook's config cell):

| Parameter | Value | Original run |
|---|---|---|
| `MAX_ROWS_PER_CONDITION` | 25 = every row in the demo data | N = 300 per baseline, 100 per ban arm and succession step, 50 per mention twin |
| `N_RANKINGS_PER_X` | 20 | `N_RANK_SAMPLES` = 20 |
| `N_FREETEXT_PER_X` | 20 | `N_FREE_SAMPLES` = 20 |
| `N_BOOT` | 2000 | 2000 |
| `N_BOOT_STABILITY` | 1000 | 1000 |

To reproduce every number from all 3,260 programs, run Path A in the experiment folder (`reproducibility.md`
there). Full regeneration (Path B) needs a GPU with at least 20 GB, vLLM 0.11 and an OpenRouter key.

## Changes to the original code (kept minimal)

- File I/O is replaced by in-memory data from `mini_demo_data.json`: `read_commitments`, `load_conditions`,
  `load_eval_cache`, `labelled_rows`, `load_events`, `assert_committed` and `commitment_audit` read the shipped
  records. The directory constants and the file sinks are dropped.
- `gen.run_vllm.run_requests` is replaced by `replay_requests`, which returns recorded rows and checks seeds.
  `check_template` and `stage_smoke` are skipped because they need the tokenizer or a GPU.
- `evaluate_unique` serves cached evaluations only. The sandbox harness (`tasks/`) is not included, and the
  function asserts that every program is cached.
- `judge_many` answers from the judge cache, under the same key `model|task|sha256(code)`.
- `ast_rules.label` and `runtime_rules.label` are kept in two namespaces, because both modules define `label`.
- Only the Qwen elicitation is rebuilt; the Llama replication is not part of the demo. The baseline objects are
  built for `baseline` only.
- The succession step-2 banned sets are taken from the frozen step-2 prediction files. The step-2 prompts are
  rebuilt and asserted equal to the frozen ones.
- `stage_score` also scores the events that were informative in the full run. A 25-row subset of an N = 100 arm
  can miss the pre-registered 20-compliant-sample rule; T1 had 38 compliant correct samples out of 100.
- The bootstrap sizes come from the config cell. The clarity strata, T9, S1, C4 and landing tables are omitted,
  because they need all 13 events.

## Restoring removed files

Nothing is marked for deletion in `.aii/manifest.yaml`, so there is nothing to restore. Two derived things live
outside this folder:

- **Notebook test environment** (not stored here): recreate it with the commands under "How to run".
- **`mini_demo_data.json`** (kept): to rebuild it, run
  `python3 tools/build_mini_demo_data.py --src <experiment folder>`. The experiment folder is the one holding
  `method.py`, `data/raw_*.jsonl`, `data/labelled.jsonl`, `data/program_eval_cache.jsonl`,
  `labeller/judge_cache.jsonl`, `predictions/`, `results/` and `commitments.log`.
````

So the\nnotebook follows the experiment's own *Path A* (exact re-analysis from the recorded generations): it **replays\nrecorded Qwen3-8B generations** and re-runs every CPU stage on them.\n\n| Stage | In the notebook |\n|---|---|\n| `prompts` | rebuilds `prompts/conditions.json` and verifies its sha256 against the hash-chained commitment log (byte-identical) |\n| `preban` / `bans` / `succession` | builds the original request plans and per-request seeds; replays the recorded rows, checking each row's seed |\n| `label` | code extraction, then the runtime rules on cached tracer probes and the AST rules on the code, the cached blind-judge labels, and `combine` |\n| `elicit` / `assoc` | ranking parsing, Borda association map, free-text mapping, baseline frequencies |\n| `freeze` | sha256 check of the 4 frozen prediction files; recomputes the association / frequency / free-text predictions and compares them with the frozen ones |\n| `score` | event outcomes, commitment audit, log-score contest, disguised recurrence, mention effects |\n\n## Layout\n\n| Path | What it is |\n|---|---|\n| `code_demo.ipynb` | The demo notebook, saved with the outputs of its last test run. |\n| `mini_demo_data.json` | Curated demo data, about 0.6 MB (see below). **Runtime.** The notebook was tested in a clean Python 3.12 venv with
`jupyter nbconvert --execute --ExecutePreprocessor.timeout=600`. All cells finish in about 16 s, including the
install cell, against a 600 s budget. The config was scaled in three rounds: rows / rankings / free text / boot /
stability boot = 2/5/5/100/100, then 10/10/10/500/500, then 25/20/20/2000/1000. Each round took 15–16 s.

## What the demo reproduces

These checks pass on the 100 replayed programs:

- The rebuilt `prompts/conditions.json` is byte-identical to the committed one (sha256 `16c46dbf…`), and the hash
  chain is valid.
- The recomputed labels and test results match the full run for **100/100** programs: 80 by runtime/AST agreement,
  20 by the cached blind judge.
- The recomputed association, frequency and free-text predictions for the two modal bans are **identical to the
  frozen, hash-committed predictions**.
- Every scored event was committed before its first generation.

| Quantity | Demo (100 programs) | Full run (3,260 programs) |
|---|---|---|
| Baseline share: T1 BFS / T2 Dijkstra | 1.00 / 1.00 | 1.00 / 1.00 |
| Modal-ban compliance: T1 ban BFS / T2 ban Dijkstra | 0.92 / 0.88 | 0.90 / 0.91 |
| Realised substitute: T1 / T2 | DFS / BF-SPFA | DFS / BF-SPFA |
| Borda association nomination: T1 / T2 | Dijkstra (clarity 0.20) / BF-SPFA | Dijkstra (clarity 0.20) / BF-SPFA |
| Log score, association / frequency: T1 | −2.817 / −1.630 | −2.817 / −1.630 |
| Log score, association / frequency: T2 | −0.755 / −1.406 | −0.755 / −1.406 |
| Mean paired association − frequency | −0.268 nats/sample, association wins 1 of 2 | −0.268, 1 of 2 |
| Top-1 hits: free text / listall / Llama map / T=1.3 frequency | 2 / 2 / 1.5 / 0.45 | 2 / 2 / 1.5 / 0.45 |
| Double-ban compliance: T1 / T2 | 0.10 / 0.00 | 0.06 / 0.00 |
| Mention effect: T1 DFS / T2 BF-SPFA | +100 pp / +100 pp | +100 pp / +100 pp |

The modal ban arms are 25-row subsets of N = 100. T1's subset has 9 compliant correct programs, below the
pre-registered informativeness threshold of 20 (the full run had 38). So, as explained in the notebook, the contest
scores the events that were informative in the full run. Within each event every compliant correct program has the
same label, so the log scores match the full run exactly.

Within each event every compliant correct program has the\nsame label, so the log scores match the full run exactly.", "originalFile": "# Where an algorithm ban sends an AI coder: demo notebook\n\nA runnable, CPU-only walk-through of `method.py` from the experiment **\"What does a ban switch the model to?\"**.\nThe experiment is a behavioural screen on Qwen/Qwen3-8B (thinking off, vLLM 0.11, T = 1.0) of two predictors of\nwhere probability mass goes when a prompt bans an algorithm:\n\n- **substitution by association**: the model's own Borda-aggregated \"standard alternative to X\" ranking;\n- **the typicality ladder**: the baseline frequency, renormalised over the surviving modes.\n\nThe screen uses two path-search tasks: T1, a grid path with 6 modes, and T2, a weighted shortest path with 5 modes.\n\n`code_demo.ipynb` keeps the original code and splits it into cells, with explanations between them. The code comes\nfrom `method.py` and the stage code it dispatches to: `common.py`, `gen/prompts.py`, `pipeline.py`,\n`labeller/{extract,ast_rules,runtime_rules,combine,judge}.py` and `analysis/{assoc,metrics,stages,score}.py`.\n\nThe generation stages need a vLLM server with Qwen3-8B on a GPU, and the judge needs an OpenRouter key. So the\nnotebook follows the experiment's own *Path A* (exact re-analysis from the recorded generations): it **replays\nrecorded Qwen3-8B generations** and re-runs every CPU stage on them.\n\n| Stage | In the notebook |\n|---|---|\n| `prompts` | rebuilds `prompts/conditions.json` and verifies its sha256 against the hash-chained commitment log (byte-identical) |\n| `preban` / `bans` / `succession` | builds the original request plans and per-request seeds; replays the recorded rows, checking each row's seed |\n| `label` | code extraction, then the runtime rules on cached tracer probes and the AST rules on the code, the cached blind-judge labels, and `combine` |\n| `elicit` / `assoc` | ranking parsing, Borda association map, free-text mapping, baseline frequencies |\n| `freeze` | sha256 check of the 4 frozen prediction files; recomputes the association / frequency / free-text predictions and compares them with the frozen ones |\n| `score` | event outcomes, commitment audit, log-score contest, disguised recurrence, mention effects |\n\n## Layout\n\n| Path | What it is |\n|---|---|\n| `code_demo.ipynb` | The demo notebook, saved with the outputs of its last test run. |\n| `mini_demo_data.json` | Curated demo data, about 0.6 MB (see below). The notebook loads it from GitHub and falls back to this local copy. |\n| `tools/build_mini_demo_data.py` | Rebuilds `mini_demo_data.json` from the experiment folder (`--src <folder>`). |\n| `README.md` | This file. |\n| `.aii/manifest.yaml` | Storage decisions. Nothing in this folder is heavy, so it has no entries. |\n\n## The demo data (`mini_demo_data.json`)\n\n`examples` holds **100 recorded program generations**, in the raw generation schema plus each row's full-run label.\nThey were picked systematically, by sample index, with no selection on outcome:\n\n| Condition | T1 | T2 |\n|---|---|---|\n| baseline | 10 | 10 |\n| modal single ban (`ban:bfs` / `ban:dijkstra`) | 25 | 25 |\n| mention twin of the realised substitute (`mention:dfs` / `mention:bf_spfa`) | 5 | 5 |\n| succession step 2, a double ban (`succession:2`) | 10 | 10 |\n\n`metadata` holds the auxiliary records the stages need:\n\n- the 40 ranking and 40 free-text elicitation rows for X = BFS (T1) and X = Dijkstra (T2);\n- the cached program evaluations (hidden tests + raw tracer probes) and the cached judge labels for these 100\n  programs;\n- the 5-entry commitment log;\n- the raw text of the 4 frozen prediction files;\n- full-run reference numbers for side-by-side comparison.\n\n## How to run\n\n**Colab:** open `code_demo.ipynb` and run all cells. The install cell installs only `loguru`; Colab's own numpy,\nscipy, pandas and matplotlib are used.\n\n**Locally** (Python 3.12):\n\n```bash\nuv venv .venv --seed --python 3.12\nuv pip install --python .venv/bin/python jupyter ipykernel\n.venv/bin/jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute code_demo.ipynb --output code_demo.ipynb\n```\n\nOff Colab, the install cell pins Colab's versions: numpy 2.0.2, scipy 1.16.3, pandas 2.2.2, matplotlib 3.10.0 and\nloguru 0.7.3.\n\n**Configuration** (the notebook's config cell):\n\n| Parameter | Value | Original run |\n|---|---|---|\n| `MAX_ROWS_PER_CONDITION` | 25 = every row in the demo data | N = 300 per baseline, 100 per ban arm and succession step, 50 per mention twin |\n| `N_RANKINGS_PER_X` | 20 | `N_RANK_SAMPLES` = 20 |\n| `N_FREETEXT_PER_X` | 20 | `N_FREE_SAMPLES` = 20 |\n| `N_BOOT` | 2000 | 2000 |\n| `N_BOOT_STABILITY` | 1000 | 1000 |\n\nTo reproduce every number from all 3,260 programs, run Path A in the experiment folder (`reproducibility.md`\nthere). Full regeneration (Path B) needs a GPU with at least 20 GB, vLLM 0.11 and an OpenRouter key.\n\n## Changes to the original code (kept minimal)\n\n- File I/O is replaced by in-memory data from `mini_demo_data.json`: `read_commitments`, `load_conditions`,\n  `load_eval_cache`, `labelled_rows`, `load_events`, `assert_committed` and `commitment_audit` read the shipped\n  records. The directory constants and the file sinks are dropped.\n- `gen.run_vllm.run_requests` is replaced by `replay_requests`, which returns recorded rows and checks seeds.\n  `check_template` and `stage_smoke` are skipped because they need the tokenizer or a GPU.\n- `evaluate_unique` serves cached evaluations only. The sandbox harness (`tasks/`) is not included, and the\n  function asserts that every program is cached.\n- `judge_many` answers from the judge cache, under the same key `model|task|sha256(code)`.\n- `ast_rules.label` and `runtime_rules.label` are kept in two namespaces, because both modules define `label`.\n- Only the Qwen elicitation is rebuilt; the Llama replication is not part of the demo. The baseline objects are\n  built for `baseline` only.\n- The succession step-2 banned sets are taken from the frozen step-2 prediction files. The step-2 prompts are\n  rebuilt and asserted equal to the frozen ones.\n- `stage_score` also scores the events that were informative in the full run. A 25-row subset of an N = 100 arm\n  can miss the pre-registered 20-compliant-sample rule; T1 had 38 compliant correct samples out of 100.\n- The bootstrap sizes come from the config cell. The clarity strata, T9, S1, C4 and landing tables are omitted,\n  because they need all 13 events.\n\n## Restoring removed files\n\nNothing is marked for deletion in `.aii/manifest.yaml`, so there is nothing to restore. ### [138] ASSISTANT · 2026-10-26 09:05:43 UTC

```
I've built `code_demo.ipynb` (56 cells) and `mini_demo_data.json` (0.57 MB). The notebook runs cleanly in the Colab-like test venv in about 16 s, well inside the 10-minute limit. It reproduces the experiment's headline numbers exactly from 100 replayed programs.

**How it works.** `method.py` mostly hands work off to other modules, and its generation stages need a vLLM server with Qwen3-8B on a GPU. So the notebook follows the experiment's own "Path A": it replays the recorded Qwen3-8B outputs and runs every CPU stage with the original code. That code comes from `method.py`, `common.py`, `gen/prompts.py`, `pipeline.py`, the labeller and the analysis modules, split into cells with explanations between sections. Three figures and a demo-vs-full-run table come at the end.

**What the run confirms:**
- The rebuilt prompt file matches the committed sha256 byte for byte, and the hash chain is valid.
- All 100 recomputed labels and test results match the full run.
- The recomputed association, frequency and free-text predictions are identical to the frozen, hash-committed ones.
- Log scores are −2.817 / −1.630 (T1) and −0.755 / −1.406 (T2). The mean paired difference is −0.268, and association wins 1 of 2 events.
- Double bans fail (compliance 0.10 / 0.00 in the demo, 0.06 / 0.00 in the full run), and mentioning a mode adds +100 pp to it.

**Data.** The 100 programs were picked by sample index, not by outcome: baseline, the two modal bans, the two double bans and two mention conditions. The file also carries the 40 ranking and 40 free-text answers, the cached test/tracer results and judge labels for these programs, the commitment log and the 4 frozen prediction files.

**Scaling.** Three rounds, 15–16 s each. The final config uses every row in the demo data plus the original values: 20 rankings, 20 free-text answers, 2000/1000 bootstrap resamples. The original row counts (N=300 per baseline, 100 per ban) need the full raw data, so they're left as a commented-out line.

Things that differ from the original or from the instructions:
- **Scoring rule.** T1's 25-row ban subset has 9 compliant correct programs, below the experiment's informativeness threshold of 20 (the full run had 38). The notebook therefore scores the events that were informative in the full run, and says so. Every compliant program in each event has the same label, so the log scores still match exactly.
- **Code changes.** Generation is replaced by replaying recorded rows (with a seed check). The sandbox evaluation uses cached results and fails loudly on a cache miss. The judge answers from its cache. Each change is commented in the cells and listed in the README.
- **Notebook creation.** I generated the notebook from a single source file with a small converter rather than adding cells one by one with NotebookEdit; the result is a standard notebook.
- **Todo list.** There was no todo-tracking tool in this session, so I tracked the six TODOs in my replies instead.
- **Trailing research brief.** I treated the creativity/interpretability text at the end of the paste as background for the project, not as part of this conversion step.

Nothing in the workspace is large, so `.aii/manifest.yaml` has no entries. The structured output is written to `.terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json`.

Files are in the workspace folder:
- code_demo.ipynb
- mini_demo_data.json
- README.md
- tools/build_mini_demo_data.py
- .aii/manifest.yaml
```
